# Request Triage

## When to use
At the very start of EVERY user request, before any delegation. Classify the request, decide the mode, ask
what you must, then run the matching flow. Do not dive into the SDD pipeline reflexively — that is exactly the
failure this skill exists to prevent (applying the heavy flow to a problem that needed a fast, clever fix).

## Step 0 — intake (always, before delegating)
1. Restate the request in one line and name the **mode** you are choosing + why.
2. **Resolvé antes de preguntar (ADR-0037)** applies here too, before any scoping question: check the
   original request, `docs/notas/`, `ai/state/decisions-log.jsonl`, and the approved spec/ADRs — a
   scoping doubt one of those already answers is resolved with `log-decision`, not asked.
3. If the request is still ambiguous on scope, risk, or intent after that check, ask **1–2 scoping
   questions** — do NOT start. Terse in execution, but interrogate up front. This is where you "stop the
   cart".
4. Only then delegate the first action of the chosen mode.

## The five modes

### 0. Consult / analysis — thinking together, no pipeline (check this FIRST)
Triggers: "qué opinás", "cómo encararías", "analizame esta idea", comparisons, design questions — any request
where the user wants analysis or a recommendation, not a code change. This is a first-class mode, not a
misclassified feature.
Flow: NO `init`, NO state file, NO pipeline. Delegate in parallel (these spawns are allowed and cheap):
- `brainstormer` — 3-6 genuinely different options with tradeoffs,
- `architect` — read-only pass over the three axes (relational vs non-relational vs vector store, API Gateway,
  deploy platform) plus design patterns / clean-architecture shape; NO ADR unless the user asks,
- `security-auditor` — design-level threat sketch, only when the idea touches auth/money/PII/external input.
Then SYNTHESIZE yourself into ONE multi-lens analysis: data model, architecture/patterns, security,
algorithms/complexity — ending with a recommendation plus runner-up. Close by asking: "¿Lo convierto en spec
(feature) o en scoped?". A consult NEVER starts the pipeline on its own; if durable learning surfaced,
delegate a `memory-scribe` note to the domain knowledge.

### 1. Feature / build — full SDD (opt-in; only when the work truly demands it)
Triggers — full SDD is chosen ONLY when at least one of these holds; otherwise drop to scoped-feature (if a
concrete risk signal is present) or quick-fix (the default lane):
- a net-new system or module (not an addition to established infrastructure),
- genuinely multi-package work (several coherent packages with dependencies),
- the request touches one of the three architecture axes (data store type, API Gateway, deploy platform)
  with no ADR covering it.
Before code, **run the scoping interrogation** (load `system-design-decisions`): what future/scale do you
expect? where centralized vs decentralized? what must be secure day one? and name these three axes
explicitly, not generically — **does persistence need vector/semantic search or does relational cover it?
does this need an API Gateway or is it a monolith with one client? where does it deploy and why (Vercel/PaaS
vs VPS/IaaS vs managed)?** Then the rigorous
**SDD → BDD → package workflow → regression tests** flow: spec → design+ADR (SDD) → acceptance
Given-When-Then (BDD) → spec challenge → user approval → package planning → package implementation with local
validations → package gates → one deep package review → consolidated repair → delta review → regression tests →
integration → judge → release → memory. Tests do NOT approve implementation; the independent package reviewer
does. This order is mandatory in feature mode; the three lanes below are the only
exceptions (scoped-feature / quick-fix / incident).

### 2. Scoped-feature — for bounded work that shows a concrete risk signal
Triggers: a **well-bounded** change on established infrastructure where at least ONE concrete risk signal is
present: money/billing, data migrations, auth/permissions/PII, a public contract or shared API, genuinely
multi-module work, or the user explicitly asking for the full treatment ("hacelo con el pipeline completo").
The canonical case: "a login view + password recovery on Supabase". Without one of these signals, a bounded
change is a quick-fix, not a scoped-feature — running spec + planner + panel + judge on an ordinary bugfix is
the same waste as running the full panel after every task (that is what turned a login into a 4-5h grind).
Flow: SDD-lite (spec + acceptance Given-When-Then; an ADR only if there is a genuinely new architectural
decision) → BDD connection point with the user → one or more coherent packages → package gates → **ONE
consolidated package review over the complete package diff** → `security-auditor` when the package
touches that surface → regression tests → `adversarial-judge` → release → memory.
What is cut: deep review does NOT run after each task or each trivial repair. Security guarantee is preserved by
reviewing the complete relevant package/diff once, then delta-reviewing repairs. Escalate to feature mode only
when one of feature mode's explicit triggers appears (net-new system, multi-package, uncovered architecture
axis) — "this feels big" is a reason to scope the package better, not to escalate.

### 3. Quick-fix — the DEFAULT for small and medium bounded work
Triggers: any small-or-medium, well-understood change with a clear blast radius — a bugfix, a bounded behavior
tweak, a change across 1-3 files, copy/config, one function or one component. This is the write-side half of
the same 1-3 number `orchestrator.md`'s "Direct-read vs. delegated-explore threshold" (ADR-0020) uses for the
read side — the two are kept as one cross-referenced constant, not two independent numbers. This is the default lane: most
day-to-day requests land here unless a concrete scoped/feature trigger is present. Flow: implement →
`gate-runner` verify → done. Skip spec/design/ADR and the full audit panel — UNLESS the diff itself turns out
to touch a concrete risk signal (auth/money/PII/migration/public contract), then **escalate to scoped-feature
or feature mode**, naming the signal. MANDATORY at close: record the minimal durable trace with
`python3 ai/scripts/feature-state.py log-quickfix --summary "<what/why>" --result done --file <path> --gate "<gate evidence>"`
— quick-fixes with no trace are how the development thread gets lost.

### 4. Incident / break-glass — production is broken NOW
Triggers: production down or a user blocked with no in-app path, and speed matters more than ceremony (e.g. a
prod user needs a password reset and there is no forgot-password flow → a one-shot `psql` script, done).
Flow: fastest correct + ingenious fix, minimal ceremony, delegate the actual change to `debugger`/`implementer`
(you still never implement yourself). MANDATORY afterward — never skip: (a) write down exactly what was done,
(b) open a follow-up task to do it properly, (c) delegate a memory note. Break-glass is a conscious, logged
exception to the rigor default — not a licence to abandon it.

Ambiguous which mode? Ask. Otherwise pick the LIGHTEST mode that covers the risk actually observed in the
request and the code — not the hypothetical risk. Escalate only when you find a concrete signal from the
lists above, and name that signal in the narration. Downgrading is equally legitimate: if mid-scoped the
planner or implementer establishes the change is smaller than assumed (no signal actually present), degrade
to quick-fix and record why with `log-decision` — escalation is not a one-way ratchet.

## Physical budgets per mode (enforced by the state CLI, not by prose)

The mode you choose is not just a flow — it sets hard budgets in the feature state. Pass it to
`feature-state.py init` via `--mode`:

| Mode | `--mode` | Spawns/package | Deep review cycles | Gate failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Feature / SDD | `feature` (opt-in) | 12 | 2 | 3 |
| Scoped-feature | `scoped` (default) | 8 | 2 | 3 |
| Quick-fix | `quick-fix` | 4 | 1 | 2 |
| Incident | `incident` | 6 | 1 | 2 |

Two physical waivers keep light modes light without prose exceptions:
- `record-repair --skip-delta` skips the delta review ONLY when every repaired finding is ≤ medium severity
  and the repair touched ≤ 3 files (the CLI rejects it otherwise, and the waiver is recorded in the event).
- `create-package --runtime-surface false` (planner declaration) lets a package with no observable runtime
  surface become accept-ready after testing, without spawning `app-runner`/`runtime-verifier`. Default is
  `true` — fail-safe.

`record-spawn` blocks the feature when the spawn budget is exhausted. If a quick-fix genuinely needs more
than 4 spawns, that is the signal it was misclassified — re-triage to `scoped`/`feature` (a conscious,
logged decision), do not fight the budget.

## Architecture red-flags (transversal — check in EVERY mode, including quick-fix)
Before delegating in ANY mode, check the request against the three named axes above: **data store type
(including vector vs relational)**, **API Gateway**, and **deploy platform (Vercel/PaaS vs VPS/IaaS)**. The
check fires on EVIDENCE, not plausibility: the request or the files it names concretely require one of these
axes (a new kind of persistence, a new external entry point, a deploy change) AND no existing ADR covers it.
When it fires, do not implement directly — escalate to at least `scoped-feature` with an architecture
checkpoint (`architect` loads `system-design-decisions`, proposes options, and the orchestrator asks the user
per its Question policy) before any code is written. This applies even to a request that looks like a
quick-fix on its surface ("add semantic search to the docs page" is a one-line ask, but it is a data-store
decision). What it does NOT mean: "could hypothetically touch persistence someday" is not a red flag — an
ordinary bugfix inside existing tables/routes/deploy stays a quick-fix. A safe default is NOT an escape
hatch for these three axes specifically — see `orchestrator.md`'s Question policy.
**Named-platform carve-out (ADR-0025)**: when the request itself names the platform ("deploy this to
Vercel", "store it in Supabase"), that axis is DECIDED — record it with `log-decision` and proceed without
asking; `architect` writes the formal ADR afterwards. The red-flag question is only for an axis the user
left genuinely open.

## Waking the dormant agents (concrete triggers, not "by risk")
These agents are permitted but easy to forget — pull them in on these triggers. The trigger is evidence in
the plan or the diff (the concrete files/paths the package owns: auth/, payments/, migrations/, ui
components, .env-adjacent config), not the topic of the request in the abstract; `package-planner` justifies
each entry in `required_reviewers` with the paths that demand it:
- **auth / money / PII / any external input** → `security-auditor` is MANDATORY before the judge (its report
  covers the attack path AND the hardening/detection plan in one pass — no separate hand-off agent).
- **any user-facing surface / new UI** → `ux-ui-designer` (brand-grade, accessible; not generic defaults).
- **the approach is genuinely open / multiple viable designs** → `brainstormer` before committing.
- **queries / lists / transactions / migrations** → covered by `package-reviewer`'s own data-integrity/
  scalability checklist (already mandatory, no separate agent).

**Cadence by mode:** in package workflow, specialized reviewers run when the package touches their surface, or
when a repair changes that surface. They do not run after every ordinary task. A final evidence pass before
`adversarial-judge` is mandatory.

## Hard logic → hosted implementer (never the local leaf)
Concurrency, atomic transactions, money/financial rules, and security-critical logic must NOT be implemented by
the local leaf model (the cheap 8B) — a weak first draft on exactly this logic triggers more strong-auditor
rework than it saves. When a task touches these, pin implementation to a **hosted** model (e.g. `openai/gpt-5.4`)
rather than the local `implementer`. Route such implementation deliberately from the package plan. The
local leaf stays for boilerplate/CRUD/UI churn, which the audit panel then reviews.

## Output of triage
State: chosen mode + one-line why, the scoping questions asked (if any), and the first delegation. In
incident mode, also name the follow-up task you will open once the fire is out.

The first delegation is announced with the dual-register narration block from `orchestrator.md`
(`Cliente:` / `Ingeniería:`), and so is every delegation after it — in every mode. In consult mode the
parallel fan-out is narrated as ONE logical instance and persisted with `feature-state.py log-narrative`,
since a consult has no feature state file.

name: package-review
description: Deep independent review of a complete package diff against approved spec, package criteria, gates, integration behavior, and relevant risk surfaces.

# Package Review

## Inputs
Approved spec/version, package plan, diff, gate results, assumptions, and known risks.

## Checks
- Correctness against covered acceptance criteria.
- Integration with existing contracts.
- Architecture and maintainability with real impact.
- Security/data/performance surfaces when touched.
- Failure paths and edge cases.
- Test gaps that would leave accepted behavior unprotected.

## Cadence
One deep review per integrated package. A second pass is focused on repair delta. Full re-review only when repair
changes architecture, public contracts, or risk surface substantially.

## Long-running commands you run yourself
Never pipe a gate/suite you are verifying through a `tail -N` pipe while waiting — silence trips the
runtime's stall watchdog. Run it as `ai/scripts/heartbeat-run.py --interval N -- <command>` (ADR-0041, see
`spawn-prompt/SKILL.md`).

   300	   `REVIEW_IDENTITY_INVALID` (3c, halt) — when in doubt, omit rather than guess.
   301	5. **Worker death.** If a spawned instance dies or is lost without reaching a terminal state, close its run
   302	   the same way as an off-lane degrade: `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id>
   303	   failure`, then continue per your retry budget (Spawn economy above).
   304	6. **Narrate the decision.** The opening narration block (`record-spawn`) and its `Ingeniería:` line must
   305	   name the decision's `route_id`/`run_id` alongside the exact outcome: which variant/lane matched (same-lane
   306	   or cross-lane redirect), which legitimate-degrade reason fired (3a), or — for a hard denial (3c) — the
   307	   precise `reason_codes` that halted delegation.
   308	7. **Permission surface.** The routing CLI (`set_agents_app.py --route-*`/`--routing-*`) is an explicitly
   309	   **MUTATING-capable** exception in your read-only permission surface, exactly like `feature-state.py`:
   310	   `--route-decide` authorizes a durable run for writer roles, and you additionally close runs you own via
   311	   `--route-dispatched`/`--route-terminal`. `claude_code_spawn.py --dispatch-writer`/`--dispatch-review`
   312	   (step 2's cross-lane redirect above) is a FOURTH such exception — a real subprocess spawn, not mere
   313	   observability — narrowly allowlisted by `coord_policy.SAFE_ARGV` with an exhaustively enumerated flag
   314	   grammar, never a free-form passthrough. Every use is narrated like any other spawn action, never silent.
   315	8. **Usage travels with every close you make directly (ADR-0045).** Never compose a bare
   316	   `--route-terminal <run_id> success|failure` when the run you are closing is one YOU dispatched and got a
   317	   real spawn result back for — this is step 7's direct-close case, never the lost/dead worker close (step 5)
   318	   or the before-dispatch abandon (3a), where the store forces `absent` regardless of what you pass and there
   319	   is nothing to attach. Never `claude_code_spawn.py --dispatch-writer`/`--dispatch-review`, `opencode_spawn.py`,
   320	   or `codex_spawn.py` either — those already attach `--usage` internally. The platform's own subagent-spawn
   321	   result is sitting in front of you with the exact token/cost numbers when you call this directly; sending
   322	   none of them is the exact defect ADR-0045 exists to close. Build `--usage` from your CURRENT host harness's
   323	   own reported numbers, in the flat vocabulary `_usage_row`/`ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` accept
   324	   (`input`/`output`/`cache_read`/`cache_write`/`reasoning` token counts, `cost.total` in dollars) — never a
   325	   shape you invented, never a bare `0` for a number you did not actually observe:
   326	   - **Claude Code host** — the Task result's usage mirrors `claude --output-format json`'s own
   327	     `modelUsage.<model>` object (`inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `cacheReadInputTokens`,
   328	     `cacheCreationInputTokens`, `costUSD`). Run exactly:
   329	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   330	     '{"input": <inputTokens>, "output": <outputTokens>, "cache_read": <cacheReadInputTokens>, "cache_write":
   331	     <cacheCreationInputTokens>, "cost": {"total": <costUSD>}}'` — never add a `totalTokens` key here: this
   332	     shape carries no reasoning-token field and no independent total to cross-check a derived sum against
   333	     (`ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` module docstring, measured live).
   334	   - **OpenCode host** — the subagent result's usage mirrors `opencode run --format json`'s `step_finish`
   335	     event, `part.tokens` (`total`, `input`, `output`, `reasoning`, `cache.read`, `cache.write`) with sibling
   336	     `part.cost`. Run exactly:
   337	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   338	     '{"input": <tokens.input>, "output": <tokens.output>, "reasoning": <tokens.reasoning>, "cache_read":
   339	     <tokens.cache.read>, "cache_write": <tokens.cache.write>, "cost": {"total": <part.cost>}}'` — never pass
   340	     `tokens.total` as `totalTokens`: measured live, it does not equal the sum of the other five fields, and
   341	     `_usage_row` would discard a genuine report as `invalid` over it.
   342	   - **Codex host** — the turn result's usage mirrors `codex exec --json`'s `turn.completed.usage`
   343	     (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `reasoning_output_tokens`). Run exactly:
   344	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   345	     '{"input": <input_tokens>, "output": <output_tokens>, "reasoning": <reasoning_output_tokens>}'` —
   346	     `cached_input_tokens`/`cache_write_input_tokens` are UNVERIFIED as additive versus already included in
   347	     `input_tokens`: omit `cache_read`/`cache_write` rather than guess, and never invent a `cost` — this
   348	     lane's JSON stream carries no dollar figure.
   349	   - **Pi host** — `set_agents_spawn.route_and_spawn` already attaches `--usage` internally whenever it
   350	     dispatches; this rule only applies if you close a pi-hosted run some OTHER way (bypassing
   351	     `route_and_spawn`), in which case pi's own wire vocabulary is already the flat shape above — pass it
   352	     through unchanged, field names verbatim.
   353	   The exact mapping above is pinned by `ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` (ADR-0045) and its tests — if a
   354	   runtime's real shape ever changes, that module's docstring sample is what has to change first, this list
   355	   follows it, never the other way around.
   356	
   357	### Decide siempre — every spawn gets a routing decision (ADR-0030)
   358	
   359	The six tiered roles above are where the decision is ENFORCED end-to-end (durable run, variants, redirect).
   360	But the routing brain accepts EVERY roster role, and the curated per-area table in `models.toml` is a

+     `cached_input_tokens`/`cache_write_input_tokens` are UNVERIFIED as additive versus already included in
+     `input_tokens`: omit `cache_read`/`cache_write` rather than guess, and never invent a `cost` — this
+     lane's JSON stream carries no dollar figure.
+   - **Pi host** — `set_agents_spawn.route_and_spawn` already attaches `--usage` internally whenever it
+     dispatches; this rule only applies if you close a pi-hosted run some OTHER way (bypassing
+     `route_and_spawn`), in which case pi's own wire vocabulary is already the flat shape above — pass it
+     through unchanged, field names verbatim.
+   The exact mapping above is pinned by `ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` (ADR-0045) and its tests — if a
+   runtime's real shape ever changes, that module's docstring sample is what has to change first, this list
+   follows it, never the other way around.
 
 ### Decide siempre — every spawn gets a routing decision (ADR-0030)
 
 The six tiered roles above are where the decision is ENFORCED end-to-end (durable run, variants, redirect).
 But the routing brain accepts EVERY roster role, and the curated per-area table in `models.toml` is a
 FALLBACK layer, not the ceiling. So, additionally:
 
 1. **Decide for every spawn**, not only the six: before delegating ANY role (analysis, docs, gate, memory,
    release included), run the same `--route-decide` with the role's real `role`/`task_class`/`risk`. For
    non-writer, non-verified-review roles the envelope comes back `simulate` — that is expected and still a
    decision: it names the provider/model/tier the brain would pick for this task, with reason codes.
    Then pass the decision into the spawn record STRUCTURED, never only as prose: `record-spawn <PKG> <role>
diff --git i/ai/scripts/routing_core/store.py w/ai/scripts/routing_core/store.py

@@ -141,24 +141,33 @@ def _usage_row(usage) -> tuple:
     itself returns `usage or {}`, so `{}` is the ordinary "provider reported nothing" case,
     not an error -- and that is `absent`, never `invalid`.
 
     Any single untrustworthy value -- a non-integer or negative token count, a `totalTokens`
     that does not match the sum of the fields actually present, an unconvertible cost --
     discards the ENTIRE usage rather than storing a partially-trusted mix:
     `usage_status='invalid'` IS the record of the discard, so nothing beneath it is kept.
 
     A field simply absent from an otherwise-good usage stays NULL and the status stays
     `'ok'` -- NULL means "not reported", 0 means "reported as zero", and Pi reporting no
     cache/reasoning keys at all is the ordinary case this whole package exists to make
     visible rather than fabricate away.
+
+    023-senales-de-consumo PKG-B1 (ADR-0045): a NON-EMPTY dict matching NONE of this
+    vocabulary -- no recognized token field, no `totalTokens`, no `cost` -- is `invalid`,
+    not `ok`. Before this, `{"unrecognized_key": 1}` silently returned the all-NULL `'ok'`
+    row: indistinguishable from `{"cost": {"total": 0}}` (AC-11's own "sparse but valid"
+    case, correctly `'ok'`), and from `absent` in every view that only looks at "are the
+    columns NULL". Tightening only -- `{}`/`None` still short-circuit to `absent` above,
+    before this check is ever reached, and every previously-`ok` shape (at least one
+    recognized field, however sparse) is untouched.
     """
     all_null = (None,) * (len(USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS) + 1)
     if not usage:
         return all_null + ("absent",)
     if not isinstance(usage, dict):
         return all_null + ("invalid",)
     tokens: dict = {}
     for field in USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS:
         key = _usage_field_key(usage, field)
         if key is None:
             continue
         value = usage[key]
@@ -170,24 +179,30 @@ def _usage_row(usage) -> tuple:
         if isinstance(total_tokens, bool) or not isinstance(total_tokens, int) or total_tokens < 0:
             return all_null + ("invalid",)
         if total_tokens != sum(tokens.values()):
             return all_null + ("invalid",)
     cost_micros = None
     if "cost" in usage:
         cost = usage["cost"]
         if not isinstance(cost, dict) or "total" not in cost:
             return all_null + ("invalid",)
         cost_micros = _cost_micros(cost["total"])
         if cost_micros is None:
             return all_null + ("invalid",)
+    # See the docstring's ADR-0045 paragraph: checked LAST, after every individual-field
+    # validity check above, so a recognized-but-bad value still reports its own specific
+    # `invalid` reason path first (unchanged) -- this only catches the case none of those
+    # paths would ever reach, a non-empty dict that recognized nothing at all.
+    if not tokens and total_tokens is None and "cost" not in usage:

+    def test_usage_row_nonempty_unrecognized_dict_is_invalid_not_ok(self):
+        """AC-02/AC-03: a NON-EMPTY dict matching NONE of the vocabulary (no token field, no
+        `totalTokens`, no `cost`) is `invalid`, never the silent all-NULL `ok` it used to be.

     2	edge -- never a second, independently-drifting copy of it.
     3	
     4	`store._usage_row` validates and stores exactly ONE flat vocabulary: `input`, `output`,
     5	`cache_read`, `cache_write`, `reasoning` (token counts), `totalTokens` (an optional caller-
     6	supplied cross-check), and `cost.total` (`store.USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS`). It stays the closed
     7	validator (never relaxed by this module -- a value this module hands it is validated exactly
     8	as strictly as one typed in by hand). Every runtime's OWN usage report is a genuinely
     9	different wire shape. This module is the one place that knows all four and translates each
    10	into the store's vocabulary, so:
    11	
    12	- the doctrine template `Global/_canonical/agents/orchestrator.md` gives the orchestrator for
    13	  a same-lane manual `--route-terminal ... --usage` close is a PROSE COPY of the mapping
    14	  defined here, not an independent guess -- if a runtime's real shape ever changes, this

    46	    {"total_cost_usd":0.0271811,"modelUsage":{"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001":{
    47	     "inputTokens":10,"outputTokens":43,"cacheReadInputTokens":18101,
    48	     "cacheCreationInputTokens":12573,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":0.0271811,
    49	     "contextWindow":200000,"maxOutputTokens":32000,"canonicalModel":"claude-haiku-4-5",
    50	     "provider":"firstParty"}}}
    51	No `reasoning`/`totalTokens` mapped: this shape carries no reasoning-token field at all (the
    52	separate, unrelated `output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens` lives on the raw top-level
    53	`usage` object, which neither the measured sample's `modelUsage` slice nor
    54	`claude_code_spawn.py`'s own capture includes), and there is no independent total in this
    55	shape to cross-check a derived sum against -- UNVERIFIED, left unmapped rather than guessed.
    56	
    57	**opencode** -- `opencode run -m opencode/nemotron-3.5-lightning-free --format json "Reply
    58	with exactly one word: hi"`, run live this package. `opencode_spawn.py` (`:255-256`) already
    59	extracts the last `step_finish` event's `part.tokens` into its own `--usage` composition
    60	(`:318-321`, as `{"tokens": tokens}`, missing the sibling `part.cost` entirely) -- captured
    61	real sample (the `part` object of that event):
    62	    {"tokens":{"total":30493,"input":30322,"output":0,"reasoning":197,
    63	     "cache":{"write":0,"read":0}},"cost":0}
    64	`tokens.total` is deliberately NOT mapped to `totalTokens`: measured live, `30493 !=
    65	30322+0+197+0+0` (`30519`) -- opencode's own "total" does not sum the same five components
    66	this vocabulary tracks (some other, unexposed accounting), so passing it through as
    67	`totalTokens` would trip `_usage_row`'s sum-mismatch check and discard a genuine report as
    68	`invalid`. This mismatch is the measured reason, not a guess.
    69	
    70	**codex** -- `codex exec --ephemeral --sandbox read-only --color never --skip-git-repo-check
    71	-m gpt-5.6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort=low --json "Reply with exactly one word: hi"`, run
    72	live this package. `codex_spawn.py` attaches NO `--usage` at all today (a distinct, separate
    73	gap this package does not fix -- out of `ALCANCE`) -- captured real sample (the
    74	`turn.completed` event's `usage` object):
    75	    {"input_tokens":16057,"cached_input_tokens":8960,"cache_write_input_tokens":0,
    76	     "output_tokens":5,"reasoning_output_tokens":0}
    77	`cached_input_tokens`/`cache_write_input_tokens` are UNVERIFIED as additive components of
    78	`input_tokens` versus already included within it (no schema reference consulted this
    79	package, and the one live sample does not disambiguate it either way) -- `cache_read`/
    80	`cache_write` are deliberately left unmapped rather than risk double- or under-counting.
    81	No `cost` field appears anywhere in this event stream -- this lane's JSON carries no dollar
    82	figure to map.
    83	
    84	## `_usage_row`'s own tightening (ADR-0045, same package)
    85	
    86	A raw dict this module cannot recognize AT ALL for a given runtime (every function below
    87	then returns `{}`) is `absent` once it reaches `_usage_row` -- correct when the runtime
    88	genuinely reported nothing. A raw dict that IS non-empty but matches none of the FLAT
    89	vocabulary's own keys is a different case -- "reported something, none of it recognized" --
    90	and `_usage_row` now marks that `invalid`, never `ok` (see `store.py`'s `_usage_row`
    91	docstring). This module's own `_propagate_unrecognized` helper exists so a per-runtime
    92	sample this package cannot map to anything (a future wire-format change, or a field this
    93	package genuinely could not verify) surfaces as `invalid` through that same path, rather
    94	than silently vanishing as `absent`.
    95	"""
    96	
    97	from __future__ import annotations
    98	
    99	RUNTIMES = ("pi", "claude-code", "opencode", "codex")
   100	
   101	
   102	def _propagate_unrecognized(raw: dict, mapped: dict) -> dict:
   103	    """`raw` was genuinely non-empty; `mapped` is what this module recognized from it. If
   104	    `mapped` ended up empty, hand `raw` itself back (unrecognized, but non-empty) so
   105	    `_usage_row`'s own tightening -- never a second copy of that rule here -- is what
   106	    turns it into `invalid`, rather than this module silently downgrading a "reported
   107	    something we didn't understand" case to `absent`."""
   108	    return mapped or dict(raw)
   109	
   110	
   111	def normalize_pi(raw: dict) -> dict:
   112	    """Identity: pi's own wire format already IS the flat vocabulary (module docstring)."""
   113	    if not isinstance(raw, dict) or not raw:
   114	        return {}
   115	    return raw
   116	
   117	
   118	def normalize_claude_code(detail: dict) -> dict:
   119	    """`detail` is the shape `claude_code_spawn.py` already extracts (`total_cost_usd` +
   120	    `modelUsage`), not the raw `claude --output-format json` object -- see module docstring
   121	    for the measured sample this maps."""
   122	    if not isinstance(detail, dict) or not detail:
   123	        return {}
   124	    model_usage = detail.get("modelUsage")
   125	    entry = next(iter(model_usage.values()), None) if isinstance(model_usage, dict) else None
   126	    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   127	        return _propagate_unrecognized(detail, {})
   128	    out: dict = {}
   129	    for src, dst in (("inputTokens", "input"), ("outputTokens", "output"),
   130	                     ("cacheReadInputTokens", "cache_read"), ("cacheCreationInputTokens", "cache_write")):
   131	        value = entry.get(src)
   132	        if isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool) and value >= 0:
   133	            out[dst] = value
   134	    cost = entry.get("costUSD", detail.get("total_cost_usd"))
   135	    if isinstance(cost, (int, float)) and not isinstance(cost, bool):
   136	        out["cost"] = {"total": cost}
   137	    return _propagate_unrecognized(detail, out)
   138	
   139	
   140	def normalize_opencode(part: dict) -> dict:
   141	    """`part` is the `step_finish` event's own `part` object (`tokens` + sibling `cost`) --
   142	    see module docstring for the measured sample this maps and why `tokens.total` is never
   143	    forwarded as `totalTokens`."""
   144	    if not isinstance(part, dict) or not part:
   145	        return {}
   146	    tokens = part.get("tokens")
   147	    out: dict = {}
   148	    if isinstance(tokens, dict):
   149	        for field in ("input", "output", "reasoning"):
   150	            value = tokens.get(field)
   151	            if isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool) and value >= 0:
   152	                out[field] = value
   153	        cache = tokens.get("cache")
   154	        if isinstance(cache, dict):
   155	            for src, dst in (("read", "cache_read"), ("write", "cache_write")):
   156	                value = cache.get(src)
   157	                if isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool) and value >= 0:
   158	                    out[dst] = value
   159	    cost = part.get("cost")
   160	    if isinstance(cost, (int, float)) and not isinstance(cost, bool):
   161	        out["cost"] = {"total": cost}
   162	    return _propagate_unrecognized(part, out)
   163	
   164	
   165	def normalize_codex(usage: dict) -> dict:
   166	    """`usage` is the `turn.completed` event's own `usage` object -- see module docstring
   167	    for the measured sample this maps and why the two cache fields stay unmapped
   168	    (UNVERIFIED inclusion semantics) and why no `cost` is ever produced."""
   169	    if not isinstance(usage, dict) or not usage:
   170	        return {}
   171	    out: dict = {}
   172	    for src, dst in (("input_tokens", "input"), ("output_tokens", "output"),
   173	                     ("reasoning_output_tokens", "reasoning")):
   174	        value = usage.get(src)
   175	        if isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool) and value >= 0:
   176	            out[dst] = value
   177	    return _propagate_unrecognized(usage, out)
   178	
   179	
   180	NORMALIZERS = {
   181	    "pi": normalize_pi,
   182	    "claude-code": normalize_claude_code,
   183	    "opencode": normalize_opencode,
   184	    "codex": normalize_codex,
   185	}
   186	
   187	
   188	def normalize(runtime: str, raw: dict) -> dict:
   189	    """`runtime` names one of `RUNTIMES`; any other value -- unknown or absent -- is
   190	    UNVERIFIED for translation, so this returns `{}` rather than guess a mapping (the
   191	    caller's own `raw` should be passed to `_usage_row` unmapped in that case, not through
   192	    here)."""
   193	    fn = NORMALIZERS.get(runtime)
   194	    if fn is None or not isinstance(raw, dict):
   195	        return {}
   196	    return fn(raw)
   133	def _usage_row(usage) -> tuple:
   134	    """AC-11: the store's half of the two-edge split -- parseable but untrustworthy usage
   135	    never aborts `close_run`; it is discarded and the discard is recorded, never silent.
   136	
   137	    Returns a 7-tuple in `USAGE_COLUMNS` order: the five nullable token counts, then
   138	    `cost_micros`, then `usage_status`.
   139	
   140	    `usage` is `None` or `{}` for the two failure closes that never spawned -- `spawn()`
   141	    itself returns `usage or {}`, so `{}` is the ordinary "provider reported nothing" case,
   142	    not an error -- and that is `absent`, never `invalid`.
   143	
   144	    Any single untrustworthy value -- a non-integer or negative token count, a `totalTokens`
   145	    that does not match the sum of the fields actually present, an unconvertible cost --
   146	    discards the ENTIRE usage rather than storing a partially-trusted mix:
   147	    `usage_status='invalid'` IS the record of the discard, so nothing beneath it is kept.
   148	
   149	    A field simply absent from an otherwise-good usage stays NULL and the status stays
   150	    `'ok'` -- NULL means "not reported", 0 means "reported as zero", and Pi reporting no
   151	    cache/reasoning keys at all is the ordinary case this whole package exists to make
   152	    visible rather than fabricate away.
   153	
   154	    023-senales-de-consumo PKG-B1 (ADR-0045): a NON-EMPTY dict matching NONE of this
   155	    vocabulary -- no recognized token field, no `totalTokens`, no `cost` -- is `invalid`,
   156	    not `ok`. Before this, `{"unrecognized_key": 1}` silently returned the all-NULL `'ok'`
   157	    row: indistinguishable from `{"cost": {"total": 0}}` (AC-11's own "sparse but valid"
   158	    case, correctly `'ok'`), and from `absent` in every view that only looks at "are the
   159	    columns NULL". Tightening only -- `{}`/`None` still short-circuit to `absent` above,
   160	    before this check is ever reached, and every previously-`ok` shape (at least one
   161	    recognized field, however sparse) is untouched.
   162	    """
   163	    all_null = (None,) * (len(USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS) + 1)
   164	    if not usage:
   165	        return all_null + ("absent",)
   166	    if not isinstance(usage, dict):
   167	        return all_null + ("invalid",)
   168	    tokens: dict = {}
   169	    for field in USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS:
   170	        key = _usage_field_key(usage, field)
   171	        if key is None:
   172	            continue
   173	        value = usage[key]
   174	        if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int) or value < 0:
   175	            return all_null + ("invalid",)
   176	        tokens[field] = value
   177	    total_tokens = usage.get("totalTokens")
   178	    if total_tokens is not None:
   179	        if isinstance(total_tokens, bool) or not isinstance(total_tokens, int) or total_tokens < 0:
   180	            return all_null + ("invalid",)
   181	        if total_tokens != sum(tokens.values()):
   182	            return all_null + ("invalid",)
   183	    cost_micros = None
   184	    if "cost" in usage:
   185	        cost = usage["cost"]
   186	        if not isinstance(cost, dict) or "total" not in cost:
   187	            return all_null + ("invalid",)
   188	        cost_micros = _cost_micros(cost["total"])
   189	        if cost_micros is None:
   190	            return all_null + ("invalid",)
   191	    # See the docstring's ADR-0045 paragraph: checked LAST, after every individual-field
   192	    # validity check above, so a recognized-but-bad value still reports its own specific
   193	    # `invalid` reason path first (unchanged) -- this only catches the case none of those
   194	    # paths would ever reach, a non-empty dict that recognized nothing at all.
   195	    if not tokens and total_tokens is None and "cost" not in usage:
   196	        return all_null + ("invalid",)
   197	    return tuple(tokens.get(field) for field in USAGE_TOKEN_FIELDS) + (cost_micros, "ok")
   198	
   300	   `REVIEW_IDENTITY_INVALID` (3c, halt) — when in doubt, omit rather than guess.
   301	5. **Worker death.** If a spawned instance dies or is lost without reaching a terminal state, close its run
   302	   the same way as an off-lane degrade: `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id>
   303	   failure`, then continue per your retry budget (Spawn economy above).
   304	6. **Narrate the decision.** The opening narration block (`record-spawn`) and its `Ingeniería:` line must
   305	   name the decision's `route_id`/`run_id` alongside the exact outcome: which variant/lane matched (same-lane
   306	   or cross-lane redirect), which legitimate-degrade reason fired (3a), or — for a hard denial (3c) — the
   307	   precise `reason_codes` that halted delegation.
   308	7. **Permission surface.** The routing CLI (`set_agents_app.py --route-*`/`--routing-*`) is an explicitly
   309	   **MUTATING-capable** exception in your read-only permission surface, exactly like `feature-state.py`:
   310	   `--route-decide` authorizes a durable run for writer roles, and you additionally close runs you own via
   311	   `--route-dispatched`/`--route-terminal`. `claude_code_spawn.py --dispatch-writer`/`--dispatch-review`
   312	   (step 2's cross-lane redirect above) is a FOURTH such exception — a real subprocess spawn, not mere
   313	   observability — narrowly allowlisted by `coord_policy.SAFE_ARGV` with an exhaustively enumerated flag
   314	   grammar, never a free-form passthrough. Every use is narrated like any other spawn action, never silent.
   315	8. **Usage travels with every close you make directly (ADR-0045).** Never compose a bare
   316	   `--route-terminal <run_id> success|failure` when the run you are closing is one YOU dispatched and got a
   317	   real spawn result back for — this is step 7's direct-close case, never the lost/dead worker close (step 5)
   318	   or the before-dispatch abandon (3a), where the store forces `absent` regardless of what you pass and there
   319	   is nothing to attach. Never `claude_code_spawn.py --dispatch-writer`/`--dispatch-review`, `opencode_spawn.py`,
   320	   or `codex_spawn.py` either — those already attach `--usage` internally. The platform's own subagent-spawn
   321	   result is sitting in front of you with the exact token/cost numbers when you call this directly; sending
   322	   none of them is the exact defect ADR-0045 exists to close. Build `--usage` from your CURRENT host harness's
   323	   own reported numbers, in the flat vocabulary `_usage_row`/`ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` accept
   324	   (`input`/`output`/`cache_read`/`cache_write`/`reasoning` token counts, `cost.total` in dollars) — never a
   325	   shape you invented, never a bare `0` for a number you did not actually observe:
   326	   - **Claude Code host** — the Task result's usage mirrors `claude --output-format json`'s own
   327	     `modelUsage.<model>` object (`inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `cacheReadInputTokens`,
   328	     `cacheCreationInputTokens`, `costUSD`). Run exactly:
   329	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   330	     '{"input": <inputTokens>, "output": <outputTokens>, "cache_read": <cacheReadInputTokens>, "cache_write":
   331	     <cacheCreationInputTokens>, "cost": {"total": <costUSD>}}'` — never add a `totalTokens` key here: this
   332	     shape carries no reasoning-token field and no independent total to cross-check a derived sum against
   333	     (`ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` module docstring, measured live).
   334	   - **OpenCode host** — the subagent result's usage mirrors `opencode run --format json`'s `step_finish`
   335	     event, `part.tokens` (`total`, `input`, `output`, `reasoning`, `cache.read`, `cache.write`) with sibling
   336	     `part.cost`. Run exactly:
   337	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   338	     '{"input": <tokens.input>, "output": <tokens.output>, "reasoning": <tokens.reasoning>, "cache_read":
   339	     <tokens.cache.read>, "cache_write": <tokens.cache.write>, "cost": {"total": <part.cost>}}'` — never pass
   340	     `tokens.total` as `totalTokens`: measured live, it does not equal the sum of the other five fields, and
   341	     `_usage_row` would discard a genuine report as `invalid` over it.
   342	   - **Codex host** — the turn result's usage mirrors `codex exec --json`'s `turn.completed.usage`
   343	     (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `reasoning_output_tokens`). Run exactly:
   344	     `python3 __SET_AGENTS_ROOT__/ai/scripts/set_agents_app.py --route-terminal <run_id> success --usage
   345	     '{"input": <input_tokens>, "output": <output_tokens>, "reasoning": <reasoning_output_tokens>}'` —
   346	     `cached_input_tokens`/`cache_write_input_tokens` are UNVERIFIED as additive versus already included in
   347	     `input_tokens`: omit `cache_read`/`cache_write` rather than guess, and never invent a `cost` — this
   348	     lane's JSON stream carries no dollar figure.
   349	   - **Pi host** — `set_agents_spawn.route_and_spawn` already attaches `--usage` internally whenever it
   350	     dispatches; this rule only applies if you close a pi-hosted run some OTHER way (bypassing
   351	     `route_and_spawn`), in which case pi's own wire vocabulary is already the flat shape above — pass it
   352	     through unchanged, field names verbatim.
   353	   The exact mapping above is pinned by `ai/scripts/routing_core/usage.py` (ADR-0045) and its tests — if a
   354	   runtime's real shape ever changes, that module's docstring sample is what has to change first, this list
   355	   follows it, never the other way around.
   356	
   357	### Decide siempre — every spawn gets a routing decision (ADR-0030)
   358	
   359	The six tiered roles above are where the decision is ENFORCED end-to-end (durable run, variants, redirect).
   360	But the routing brain accepts EVERY roster role, and the curated per-area table in `models.toml` is a
  2590	                                                                  # do not map
  2591	            {"cost": {"total": "free"}},
  2592	            {"cost": {}},
  2593	            {"cost": "0.01"},
  2594	        ]
  2595	        for usage in cases:
  2596	            self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row(usage), all_null_invalid, msg=repr(usage))
  2597	
  2598	    # ---- 023-senales-de-consumo PKG-B1 (ADR-0045): _usage_row tightening + usage.py normalizers
  2599	
  2600	    def test_usage_row_nonempty_unrecognized_dict_is_invalid_not_ok(self):
  2601	        """AC-02/AC-03: a NON-EMPTY dict matching NONE of the vocabulary (no token field, no
  2602	        `totalTokens`, no `cost`) is `invalid`, never the silent all-NULL `ok` it used to be.
  2603	        This is a tightening, not a relaxation -- every case here previously returned `ok`.
  2604	        """
  2605	        all_null_invalid = (None, None, None, None, None, None, "invalid")
  2606	        cases = [
  2607	            {"unrecognized_key": 1},
  2608	            {"modelUsage": {"m": {"someWeirdField": 5}}},  # a raw, un-normalized claude-code
  2609	                                                              # detail with nothing recognized
  2610	            {"tokens": {"total": 5}},  # a raw opencode `part` with no input/output/reasoning/cache
  2611	            {"foo": "bar", "baz": [1, 2, 3]},
  2612	        ]
  2613	        for usage in cases:
  2614	            self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row(usage), all_null_invalid, msg=repr(usage))
  2615	
  2616	    def test_usage_row_absent_vs_invalid_boundary_is_emptiness_not_recognition(self):
  2617	        """The exact boundary the tightening above must never cross: `{}`/`None` (genuinely
  2618	        nothing reported) stay `absent`; a non-empty dict of the same "nothing recognized"
  2619	        shape is `invalid`. Placed side by side so a future edit that merges the two branches
  2620	        is caught here, not just in the two tests separately.
  2621	        """
  2622	        all_null_absent = (None, None, None, None, None, None, "absent")
  2623	        all_null_invalid = (None, None, None, None, None, None, "invalid")
  2624	        self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row({}), all_null_absent)
  2625	        self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row({"anything": 1}), all_null_invalid)
  2626	
  2627	    def test_usage_row_still_ok_for_every_previously_valid_sparse_shape(self):
  2628	        """Regression guard for the tightening above: every shape that recognizes AT LEAST
  2629	        one field (however sparse) is completely unaffected -- `ok`, same values as before.
  2630	        """
  2631	        self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row({"input": 5}),
  2632	                         (5, None, None, None, None, None, "ok"))
  2633	        self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row({"totalTokens": 0}),
  2634	                         (None, None, None, None, None, None, "ok"))
  2635	        self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row({"cost": {"total": decimal.Decimal("0")}}),
  2636	                         (None, None, None, None, None, 0, "ok"))

  2639	    # docstring for the exact commands run and the full captured JSON these are extracted

  2660	    def test_usage_normalize_claude_code_real_sample_yields_nonnull_columns(self):
  2661	        mapped = routing_usage.normalize_claude_code(self._CLAUDE_CODE_SAMPLE)
  2662	        self.assertEqual(mapped, {"input": 10, "output": 43, "cache_read": 18101,
  2663	                                  "cache_write": 12573, "cost": {"total": 0.0271811}})

  2689	    def test_usage_normalize_propagates_unrecognized_nonempty_input_as_invalid(self):
  2690	        """AC-03: a non-empty per-runtime raw dict this module cannot map to ANYTHING must
  2691	        still surface as `invalid` once it reaches `_usage_row` -- never silently `absent`.
  2692	        """
  2693	        cases = [
  2694	            routing_usage.normalize_claude_code({"modelUsage": {"m": {"someWeirdField": 5}}}),
  2695	            routing_usage.normalize_opencode({"tokens": {"total": 5}}),
  2696	            routing_usage.normalize_codex({"weird_field": 123}),
  2697	            routing_usage.normalize_pi({"weird_field": 1}),
  2698	        ]
  2699	        for mapped in cases:
  2700	            self.assertTrue(mapped)  # non-empty: propagated, not silently dropped
  2701	            self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row(mapped)[-1], "invalid", msg=repr(mapped))
  2702	
  2703	    def test_usage_normalize_genuinely_empty_input_stays_absent(self):
  2704	        """The mirror of the test above: a runtime reporting LITERALLY nothing (`{}`/`None`)
  2705	        must still normalize to `{}` and reach `_usage_row` as `absent`, never `invalid`.
  2706	        """
  2707	        for fn in (routing_usage.normalize_claude_code, routing_usage.normalize_opencode,
  2708	                   routing_usage.normalize_codex, routing_usage.normalize_pi):
  2709	            self.assertEqual(fn({}), {})
  2710	            self.assertEqual(fn(None), {})
  2711	            self.assertEqual(routing_store._usage_row(fn({}))[-1], "absent")
  2712	
  2713	    def test_usage_normalize_dispatch_table_covers_all_four_runtimes(self):
  2714	        self.assertEqual(set(routing_usage.RUNTIMES),
  2715	                         {"pi", "claude-code", "opencode", "codex"})

  2717	                         routing_usage.normalize_claude_code(self._CLAUDE_CODE_SAMPLE))

  2719	
  2720	    def test_report_status_counts_before_and_after_on_a_fixture_store_per_runtime(self):
  2721	        """AC-03: `status_counts` measured BEFORE any run closes (empty fixture store) and
  2722	        AFTER closing one run per real runtime sample (three `ok`), one genuine `absent`
  2723	        (no usage passed), and one `invalid` (a non-empty, unrecognized raw dict) -- proof
  2724	        by state transition, not by assertion alone.
  2725	        """
  2726	        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
  2727	            svc = self.service(Path(td) / "s")
  2728	            before = svc.store.report()["tokens"]["status_counts"]
  2729	            self.assertEqual(before, {})
  2730	
  2731	            def close(runtime_sample, normalizer, outcome="success"):
  2732	                decision = self.authorize(svc)
  2733	                svc.store.mark_dispatched(decision.run_id)
  2734	                mapped = normalizer(runtime_sample) if normalizer is not None else None
  2735	                svc.store.close_run(decision.run_id, outcome, usage=mapped)
  2736	
  2737	            close(self._CLAUDE_CODE_SAMPLE, routing_usage.normalize_claude_code)
  2738	            close(self._OPENCODE_SAMPLE, routing_usage.normalize_opencode)
  2739	            close(self._CODEX_SAMPLE, routing_usage.normalize_codex)
  2740	            close(None, None, outcome="failure")  # genuinely never reported -> absent
  2741	            close({"junk": 1}, lambda raw: raw, outcome="success")  # unrecognized -> invalid
  2742	
  2743	            after = svc.store.report()["tokens"]["status_counts"]
  2744	            self.assertEqual(after, {"ok": 3, "absent": 1, "invalid": 1})
  2745
